## Verified by direct fetch (2026-10-16) — pipeline optimizer (fix catalogue and measurement fields: `pipeline_profile.py`, `pipeline-optimizer.md`)

Every page was fetched on 2026-10-16 with a plain HTTP client (HTTPS URLs, no JavaScript), tags stripped, and each quoted phrase below was checked to occur verbatim in the page text (whitespace-normalised). Where a page shows a gap next to inline code, the quote stops before it. A saving the script prints is computed from the repo's own measurements; these sources only back the mechanism each fix relies on, never a number.

| Standard / source | URL | What was confirmed |
|---|---|---|
| GitHub Docs — Managing a merge queue | https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/configuring-branches-and-merges-in-your-repository/configuring-pull-request-merges/managing-a-merge-queue | "A merge queue helps increase velocity by automating pull request merges into a busy branch and ensuring the branch is never broken by incompatible changes."; it "does not require a pull request author to update their pull request branch and wait for status checks to finish before trying to merge"; "You must use the merge_group event to trigger your GitHub Actions workflow when a pull request is added to a merge queue."; a minimum group size "is useful if merges to your base branch trigger a lengthy CI build or deploy process". |
| GitHub Docs — Dependency caching reference | https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/dependency-caching | "On a cache miss, the action automatically creates a new cache if the job completes successfully."; "GitHub will remove any cache entries that have not been accessed in over 7 days."; "By default, the limit is 10 GB per repository". |
| actions/cache source, `restoreImpl.ts` | https://raw.githubusercontent.com/actions/cache/main/src/restoreImpl.ts | The restore step logs `Cache restored from key: ${cacheKey}` on a hit and `Cache not found for input keys: ...` on a miss, and a comment says `cache-hit` is intentionally not set to `false` on a miss. The hit rate therefore comes from step logs (the strings `cache_metrics` counts), not from a job field. |
| GitHub Docs — REST workflow jobs | https://docs.github.com/en/rest/actions/workflow-jobs | The example job object carries `"started_at": "2020-01-20T17:42:40Z", "completed_at": "2020-01-20T17:44:39Z"` and a `steps` array whose items carry `"name": "Set up job", "status": "completed", "conclusion": "success", "number": 1, "started_at"` and their own `completed_at`. The page text has no `created_at` for a job: queue wait is read from the live response, see the next row. |
| GitHub REST API, live response (observed, not a docs page) | https://api.github.com/repos/protocol/perun/actions/runs/{run_id}/jobs | Observed 2026-10-16 in the profiled response (30 runs, their jobs): every job carries `created_at`, `started_at`, `completed_at`, and every step `number`, `started_at`, `completed_at`. The script reads queue wait as `started_at` minus `created_at`; a job without `created_at` counts as 0 s of queue wait (a floor, not an estimate). The docs example above omits the field, so this row is an observation, not a documented guarantee. |
| GitHub Docs — REST workflow runs | https://docs.github.com/en/rest/actions/workflow-runs | "per_page integer The number of results per page (max 100)."; run objects carry `"run_attempt": 1, "run_started_at": "2020-01-22T19:33:08Z"` and a `head_commit` with a `message`; "You can use the REST API to view, re-run, cancel, and view logs for workflow runs in GitHub Actions." |
| GitHub Docs — Rate limits for the REST API | https://docs.github.com/en/rest/using-the-rest-api/rate-limits-for-the-rest-api | "The primary rate limit for unauthenticated requests is 60 requests per hour."; "If you exceed your primary rate limit, you will receive a 403 or 429 response, and the x-ratelimit-remaining header will be 0". |
| GitHub Docs — Larger runners | https://docs.github.com/en/actions/concepts/runners/larger-runners | "Larger runners are managed virtual machines with more resources than standard GitHub-hosted runners"; the feature list starts "More RAM, CPU, and disk space". |
| GitHub Docs — GitHub-hosted runners reference | https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/runners/github-hosted-runners | "Use of the standard GitHub-hosted runners is free and unlimited on public repositories."; the public-repository table lists `Linux 4 16 GB 14 GB x64 ubuntu-latest`, i.e. 4 CPUs and 16 GB (the private-repository table lists 2 CPUs and 8 GB), which is why the shard fix defaults to 4 jobs. |
| GitHub Docs — Running variations of jobs in a workflow (matrix) | https://docs.github.com/en/actions/how-tos/write-workflows/choose-what-workflows-do/run-job-variations | "A matrix strategy lets you use variables in a single job definition to automatically create multiple job runs"; "If any of the jobs with continue-on-error: false fail, all jobs that are in progress or queued will be cancelled." |
| Martin Fowler — Continuous Integration | https://martinfowler.com/articles/continuousIntegration.html | "The commit build is the one that has to be done quickly, as a result it will take a number of shortcuts that will reduce the ability to detect bugs."; "If the secondary build detects a bug, that's a sign that the commit build could do with another test."; "For most projects, however, the XP guideline of a ten minute build is perfectly within reason." |
| Software Engineering at Google, ch. 23 Continuous Integration | https://abseil.io/resources/swe-book/html/ch23.html | "CI should optimize quicker, more reliable tests on presubmit and slower, less deterministic tests on post-submit."; "Our general rule of thumb is: only fast, reliable ones."; "we typically limit presubmit tests to just those for the project where the change is happening."; "After a change has been submitted, we use TAP to asynchronously run all potentially affected tests, including larger and slower tests." |
| Google Testing Blog — Flaky Tests at Google and How We Mitigate Them | https://testing.googleblog.com/2016/05/flaky-tests-at-google-and-how-we.html | "We define a "flaky" test result as a test that exhibits both a passing and a failing result with the same code."; "A tool that monitors the flakiness of tests and if the flakiness is too high, it automatically quarantines the test."; "For projects that see lots of flaky test failures, we currently re-run failing tests one time and only report as a failure if it fails twice in a row." |
| pytest-xdist — Running tests across multiple CPUs (distribution) | https://pytest-xdist.readthedocs.io/en/stable/distribution.html | `--dist load` (default): "Sends pending tests to any worker that is available, without any guaranteed order." |
| Playwright — Sharding | https://playwright.dev/docs/test-sharding | "Sharding in Playwright means splitting your tests into smaller parts called "shards"."; "In a CI pipeline, each shard can run as a separate job, making use of the hardware resources available in your CI pipeline, like CPU cores, to run tests faster." |
| Predictive Test Selection (Machalica, Samylkin, Porth, Chandra; arXiv 1810.05286) | https://arxiv.org/abs/1810.05286 | The abstract says the learned strategy "reduces the total infrastructure cost of testing code changes by a factor of two". Used only as evidence that selecting a subset of tests per change is a recognised lever, not for any number the script prints. |
| ESLint — Command Line Interface Reference | https://eslint.org/docs/latest/use/command-line-interface | `--cache`: "Store the info about processed files in order to only operate on the changed ones." |
| TypeScript — TSConfig reference | https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/ | `incremental`: "Tells TypeScript to save information about the project graph from the last compilation to files stored on disk." |
| Ruff — Settings | https://docs.astral.sh/ruff/settings/ | `cache-dir`: "By default, Ruff stores cache results in a .ruff_cache directory in the current project root." |
| mypy — The mypy daemon | https://mypy.readthedocs.io/en/stable/mypy_daemon.html | "running mypy using the mypy daemon can be 10 or more times faster than the regular command-line mypy tool" |
| Git — githooks | https://git-scm.com/docs/githooks | pre-push: "This hook is called by git-push[1] and can be used to prevent a push from taking place."; pre-commit "can be bypassed with the --no-verify option". |
| DORA — Software delivery performance metrics | https://dora.dev/guides/dora-metrics/ | Change lead time: "The amount of time it takes for a change to go from committed to version control to deployed in production." The script measures first push to merge only, a proxy for the first leg. |
| Wikipedia — Amdahl's law | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amdahl%27s_law | "the overall performance improvement gained by optimizing a single part of a system is limited by the fraction of time that the improved part is actually used."; "even with an infinite number of processors, the speedup is constrained by the unparallelizable portion." |
| Wikipedia — Critical path method | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Critical_path_method | "is an algorithm for scheduling a set of project activities."; a critical path is determined by identifying "the longest stretch of dependent activities". |

## Verified by direct fetch (2026-10-16) — auto-compact + SessionStart re-inject (operating layer `autoCompactWindow`, `handoff_inject.py`)

Pages were fetched with a summarising fetch tool on 2026-10-16; each row records only what the summary quoted from the page. The `settings-reference` page is long, so the `autoCompactWindow` row was read from its second 100000-character slice.

| Standard / source | URL | What was confirmed |
|---|---|---|
| Claude Code — Settings reference | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/settings-reference.md | `autoCompactWindow`: scope "Any file"; type number of tokens from `100000` to `1000000` (capped at the model's context window); default unset, so Claude Code picks a window tuned for the model; `--autocompact` overrides it for one session and `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW` takes precedence over both; a per-model `modelSettings` value beats a top-level `autoCompactWindow` in the same file. The page's `modelSettings` entry also allows `"auto"`, but the top-level entry quotes no `"auto"`. |
| Claude Code — Hooks reference | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks.md | SessionStart matchers `startup`, `resume`, `clear`, `compact` ("Auto or manual compaction"), `fork`; input has `source`; plain-text stdout of a SessionStart hook is added to Claude's context; `hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext` is wrapped in a system reminder at the start of the conversation. |
| Claude Code — Context window | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/context-window.md | "What survives compaction": SessionStart hooks that match the `compact` source run after compaction and their output is added to the compacted context; Claude Code compacts automatically as the window fills; compaction replaces the conversation with a summary. |

## Verified by direct fetch (2026-10-16) — SubagentStop transcript fields and transcript format (`handback_cap.py` lane audit)

Pages were fetched with a summarising fetch tool on 2026-10-16; each row records only what the summary quoted from the page. The hooks reference is long: the `SubagentStop` section was read from its second 100000-character slice, the matcher and parallel-execution rules from its first.

| Standard / source | URL | What was confirmed |
|---|---|---|
| Claude Code — Hooks reference (`transcript_path`, `SubagentStop`) | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks | Common input field `transcript_path`: "Path to conversation JSON. The transcript file is written asynchronously and may lag the in-memory conversation, so it may not yet include the current turn's most recent messages when a hook fires. Hooks that need the final assistant text of the current turn should use `last_assistant_message` on Stop and SubagentStop instead of reading the transcript." The `SubagentStop` input example carries `transcript_path`, `agent_id`, `agent_type`, `agent_transcript_path` (example `~/.claude/projects/.../abc123/subagents/agent-def456.jsonl`) and `last_assistant_message`; "The `transcript_path` is the main session's transcript, while `agent_transcript_path` is the subagent's own transcript stored in a nested `subagents/` folder." So a lane audit reads `agent_transcript_path`, never `transcript_path`. The page gives no entry format for the transcript; the only hint is the `.jsonl` extension. Cross-checked 2026-10-16 against the hook-input schema inside the installed Claude Code 2.1.301 binary: on `SubagentStop`, `agent_transcript_path` is a required string and `last_assistant_message` is optional. |
| Claude Code — Hooks reference (matchers, parallel execution) | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks | "All matching hooks run in parallel." A matcher of only letters, digits, `_`, `-`, spaces, `,` and `|` is an exact string or list of exact strings; one that "Contains any other character" is a JavaScript regular expression, tested unanchored with `RegExp.prototype.test`. The `SubagentStop` matcher filters on agent type, "same values as `SubagentStart`". The page does not mention lookaheads; a negative lookahead is standard JavaScript regex syntax. Backs the two-entry handback-cap setup in `host-enforcement.md` (the default entry must exclude the type that gets a raised cap, because a block from either entry wins). |
| Claude Code — Manage sessions | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sessions.md | "By default, Claude Code stores transcripts as JSONL at `~/.claude/projects/<project>/<session-id>.jsonl`"; "Each line is a JSON object for a message, tool use, or metadata entry. The entry format is internal to Claude Code and changes between versions, so scripts that parse these files directly can break on any release." Hence the audit parses defensively and reports `COULD_NOT_CHECK` for a file it cannot read. The `text` and `tool_use` block shape it reads was observed on local transcripts on 2026-10-16; it is not documented. |
| Claude Code — Persist sessions to external storage | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/session-storage.md | Subagent transcripts are mirrored under `subpath: "subagents/agent-<id>"` and the subpath "follows the on-disk layout"; "Entries are JSON-safe objects, one per line in the local JSONL." Backs the derived `<session>/subagents/agent-<agent_id>.jsonl` fallback when `agent_transcript_path` is absent. |

The commands pages of the other six hosts were also fetched through a summarising fetch tool, which gave the same rows wherever it reached them. `context_nudge.py` lists only commands shown below, and `scripts/test-host-command-hints.sh` pins that per host. The `/model` and `/effort` rows refer to a confirmation step and a cache warning. |

The cache cost of a model switch is in the earlier "Claude Code — How Claude Code uses prompt caching" row (`code.claude.com/docs/en/prompt-caching`). |

"Built-in slash commands" table: `/compact` "Summarize the visible chat to free tokens."; `/clear` "Clear the terminal and start a fresh chat."; `/new` "Start a new chat inside the same CLI session."; `/model` "Choose the active model (and reasoning effort, when available)."; `/resume` "Resume a saved chat from your session list."; `/status` "Display session configuration and token usage."; `/usage` "View account token usage or use a rate-limit reset." `/reasoning` "Choose the reasoning effort for the current chat." appears only under "Extension commands" / "Available slash commands" (the IDE extension composer), so it is not used for the CLI. (`/model set <model-name>`); `/stats` "Display detailed statistics for the current Gemini CLI session." (`model` sub-command: token counts and quota); `/resume` "Browse and resume previous conversation sessions" (`/chat` is an alias). No effort or thinking command; no `/compact`, `/usage` or `/context`. |

(`/new`, `/reset`); `/compact` "Summarize the conversation history to reduce context window usage."; `/context` "Show the context window token usage and visualization."; `/model` (`/models`) "changes the model, reasoning effort, or context window for the current session only"; `/resume` (`/continue`) "Switch to a different session by choosing from a list"; `/usage` "Display session usage metrics and statistics, including per-model token totals." `--effort=LEVEL` is a launch flag; no `/effort` slash command. ## Verified by direct fetch (2026-10-16) — Claude Code hooks: main session vs subagent, per-turn id, PreToolUse and UserPromptSubmit output (`ceo_altitude_guard.py`)

Sourced for `agentic-delivery/scripts/ceo_altitude_guard.py`. The page is about 270000 characters; it was fetched on 2026-10-16 with a summarising fetch tool in 100000-character slices, and the last slice was searched only for `agent_id` and `agent_type` (no match). Each row records only what the summary quoted from the page.

| Standard / source | URL | What was confirmed |
|---|---|---|
| Claude Code — Hooks reference, common input fields (`agent_id`, `agent_type`) | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks.md | "`agent_id` and `agent_type` tell your script which agent a hook fired in, such as a subagent, an in-process teammate, or the agent you chose with `--agent`". `agent_id`: "Unique identifier for the subagent or in-process teammate the hook fires in." `agent_type`: "Present when the session uses `--agent` or the hook fires inside a subagent." Hooks in settings files "also run inside subagents. When a subagent calls a tool, tool events such as `PreToolUse` and `PostToolUse` fire the same configured hooks as in the main conversation, and the input carries the `agent_id` and `agent_type` common input fields that identify the subagent." So `agent_id` marks a subagent or teammate, and `agent_type` alone cannot tell a subagent from a main session started with `--agent`. This fetch did not return a sentence saying outright that the main session omits `agent_id`; the 2026-09-24 row above quotes one ("Present only when the hook fires inside a subagent call. Use this to distinguish subagent hook calls from main-thread calls") that this fetch did not return. The guard therefore treats "no `agent_id`" as the main session and, if that is wrong, goes silent instead of blocking a lane. |
| Claude Code — Hooks reference, common input fields (`prompt_id`) | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks.md | `prompt_id`: "UUID identifying the user prompt currently being processed. Matches the `prompt.id` attribute on OpenTelemetry events ... Absent until the first user input". The page does not say how often it changes beyond that; the guard uses it as the per-turn key and also resets the turn at `UserPromptSubmit`. |
| Claude Code — Hooks reference, PreToolUse decision control | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks.md | `PreToolUse` input adds `tool_name`, `tool_input`, `tool_use_id`. `hookSpecificOutput.permissionDecision` takes `allow`, `deny`, `ask` or `defer`; `"deny"` "prevents the tool call" and `permissionDecisionReason` is "shown to Claude" for `deny`. `additionalContext` is a "String added to Claude's context alongside the tool result". Precedence across hooks: "`deny` > `defer` > `ask` > `allow`". Exit 0 with no output "means the hook has no decision to report"; exit 2 blocks (stderr is the reason) even when JSON is printed; a timed-out `PreToolUse` command hook "doesn't block the tool call". |
| Claude Code — Hooks reference, UserPromptSubmit | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks.md | `UserPromptSubmit` hooks receive the `prompt` field "containing the submitted text"; the event also "fires on turns Claude Code starts on its own". On exit 0, plain-text stdout and `additionalContext` are added to Claude's context (so a hook that must stay silent prints nothing); exit 2 blocks the prompt. |
| Claude Code — Hooks reference, SubagentStart / SubagentStop | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks.md | `SubagentStart` and `SubagentStop` hooks receive `agent_id` and `agent_type`; `SubagentStop` also receives `agent_transcript_path` and `last_assistant_message`. "Not every SubagentStop event comes from a subagent Claude spawned": for Claude Code's own internal agents `agent_type` is the agent name the session runs as, "and an empty string when the session runs without one". |

## Verified by direct fetch (2026-10-16) — GitHub Actions prices, free minutes, skip rules and filter patterns (`ci_cost_advisor.py`, policy key `ci`)

Sourced for the rates, plan allowances and runner-label table in `scripts/ci_cost_advisor.py`, its path-filter
evaluation and its `[skip ci]` advice. Every row below was fetched on 2026-10-16 as the page's Markdown source
(docs.github.com article API); the numbers in `RATES` and `PLANS` equal the tables quoted here
(`scripts/test_ci_cost_advisor.py` pins that).

| Standard / source | URL | What was confirmed |
|---|---|---|
| GitHub Docs — Actions runner pricing | https://docs.github.com/en/billing/reference/actions-runner-pricing | Verbatim: "GitHub rounds the minutes and partial minutes each job uses up to the nearest whole minute." Per-minute rates (USD): Linux 1-core (x64) `actions_linux_slim` $0.002; Linux 2-core (x64) `actions_linux` $0.006; Linux 2-core (arm64) `actions_linux_arm` $0.005; Windows 2-core (x64 and arm64) $0.010; macOS 3-core or 4-core (M1 or Intel) `actions_macos` $0.062. Larger runners have their own rates (for example Linux 4-core $0.012, macOS 12-core $0.077). Verbatim: "Included minutes cannot be used for larger runners." and "The larger runners are not free for public repositories." |
| GitHub Docs — GitHub Actions billing | https://docs.github.com/en/billing/concepts/product-billing/github-actions | Verbatim: "GitHub Actions usage is free for self-hosted runners and for public repositories that use standard GitHub-hosted runners." Private repositories get a monthly quota of standard-runner minutes by plan: GitHub Free 2,000; GitHub Pro 3,000; GitHub Free for organizations 2,000; GitHub Team 3,000; GitHub Enterprise Cloud 50,000; "Your free minutes reset to the full amount at the start of each billing cycle", and minutes are "charged to the repository owner", so the allowance is shared by every private repository on the account. With no payment method on file, usage is blocked once the quota is used. Worked example: GitHub Team, 5,000 minutes beyond the quota (3,000 Linux, 2,000 Windows) = $38 ($18 + $20). The page does not say how Windows or macOS minutes draw down the quota, so `ci_cost_advisor.py` values included minutes at the Linux 2-core rate and calls its "billed" figure a lower bound. |
| GitHub Docs — Billing and usage (Actions) | https://docs.github.com/en/actions/concepts/billing-and-usage | Same free-usage statement for public repositories and self-hosted runners. "If you reuse a workflow, billing is always associated with the caller workflow", so a job that only calls a reusable workflow is not priced from the caller's file. |
| GitHub Docs — GitHub-hosted runners reference | https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/runners/github-hosted-runners | The standard-runner label table for private repositories: `ubuntu-slim` (1 CPU, a container on a shared VM, also available in public repositories); `ubuntu-latest`, `ubuntu-24.04`, `ubuntu-22.04`, `ubuntu-26.04`; `windows-latest`, `windows-2025`, `windows-2022`; `ubuntu-24.04-arm`, `ubuntu-22.04-arm`, `ubuntu-26.04-arm`; `windows-11-arm`, `windows-11-vs2026-arm`; `macos-15-intel`, `macos-26-intel`; `macos-latest`, `macos-14`, `macos-15`, `macos-26`, `xcode-27` (public preview). "These runners use your GitHub account's allotment of free minutes, and are then charged at the per minute rates." Any other label (a larger runner, a runner group, a custom name) is left unpriced by the advisor. |
| GitHub Docs — Skipping workflow runs | https://docs.github.com/en/actions/how-tos/manage-workflow-runs/skip-workflow-runs | Workflows triggered by `push` or `pull_request` are not triggered when the commit message of a push, or the HEAD commit of a pull request, contains `[skip ci]`, `[ci skip]`, `[no ci]`, `[skip actions]` or `[actions skip]` (or a `skip-checks: true` trailer); the instructions apply to those two events only. A workflow skipped this way, or by path or branch filtering, leaves its checks in a "Pending" state, and "A pull request that requires those checks to be successful will be blocked from merging." |
| GitHub Docs — Troubleshooting required status checks | https://docs.github.com/en/pull-requests/collaborating-with-pull-requests/collaborating-on-repositories-with-code-quality-features/troubleshooting-required-status-checks | Table "Handling skipped but required checks": a workflow skipped by path filtering, branch filtering or a commit message leaves its checks "Pending" and "block[s] merging" (fix: "Avoid requiring workflows that can be skipped"); a job skipped by a conditional "reports 'Success'". Successful check statuses are `success`, `skipped` and `neutral`. Basis for never proposing a path filter or `[skip ci]` for a required workflow. |
| GitHub Docs — Workflow syntax for GitHub Actions | https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/workflow-syntax | `paths`/`paths-ignore`: "When all the path names match patterns in `paths-ignore`, the workflow will not run. If any path names do not match patterns in `paths-ignore`, even if some path names match the patterns, the workflow will run."; in `paths` a matching `!` pattern after a positive match excludes the path and a later positive match includes it again; "Path patterns must match the whole path, and start from the repository's root"; with no changed files the workflow does not run; path filters are not evaluated for tag pushes. Filter pattern cheat sheet: `*` zero or more characters but not `/`; `**` zero or more of any character; `?` zero or one of the preceding character; `+` one or more of the preceding character; `[]` a character class; `!` at the start negates. `cancel-in-progress` may be an expression (`${{ !contains(github.ref, 'release/')}}` is the page's example). `jobs.<job_id>.timeout-minutes` default: 360. |

**By name only (observed, not stated in the fetched docs):** an unauthenticated `HEAD https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>`
answers 200 for a public repository (observed for this repository) and 404 for one that does not exist (observed);
`ci_cost_advisor.py` reads 404 as "private or missing" and treats it as private, the billable case, but a private
repository was not probed this session. The measured job times used to illustrate the advisor on this repository
(the `gates` job about 14 minutes, the release job 7 to 16 seconds) were read from the public Actions run lists at
github.com/protocol/perun/actions on 2026-10-16.

| Tool | URL | What was confirmed |

Named finding categories: Requirement gaps ("Catch PRs that miss their linked spec"), UX deviations (drift from a linked Figma design), Cross-repository conflicts (breaking changes in related repositories), Skill-related findings; a review effort mode. | Sourcery | https://docs.sourcery.ai/reviews/anatomy-of-a-review/ | Each comment starts with a kind label (examples `issue (bug_risk):` and `nitpick (performance):`); security comments carry a 🚨 marker; blocking security findings fail the status check. No complete category list or severity scale on the page. |
| SonarQube Server | https://docs.sonarsource.com/sonarqube-server/2025.4/user-guide/rules/overview and https://docs.sonarsource.com/sonarqube-server/latest/core-concepts/clean-code/software-qualities/ | Software qualities Security, Reliability, Maintainability (MQR mode categorises rules by them); Standard Experience rule types code smell, bug, vulnerability, security hotspot; coding attributes consistency, intentionality, adaptability, responsibility; impact severities Blocker, High, Medium, Low, Info. The definition page (https://docs.sonarsource.com/sonarqube-server/latest/core-concepts/clean-code/definition/) names sub-attributes including Efficient ("uses resources without needless waste"), Lawful ("respects licensing and copyright regulation"), Trustworthy ("abstains from revealing or hard-coding private information") and Respectful ("refrains from using discriminatory and offensive language"); the fetch summary mislabelled one definition, so no other sub-attribute text is relied on. |
| GitHub Copilot code review | https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/how-tos/use-copilot-agents/request-a-code-review/use-code-review and https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/concepts/agents/code-review | Comments carry a severity of High, Medium or Low. Effort levels: Lite ("targeted feedback on glaring issues such as bugs, security vulnerabilities, and style inconsistencies") and Balanced (deeper analysis of complex logic, security-sensitive code and cross-service changes). Steered by `.github/copilot-instructions.md`, path-specific `.github/instructions/*.instructions.md` and `AGENTS.md`. Leaves a "Comment" review by default, not an approval. No complete category list. |
| Anthropic code-review plugin | https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code/blob/main/plugins/code-review/README.md | Four parallel agents (two CLAUDE.md compliance, one bug scan limited to the changes, one git blame and history); each issue scored 0 to 100, default threshold 80. Filtered as false positives: pre-existing issues, code that looks like a bug but is not, pedantic nitpicks, issues linters will catch, general quality issues unless in CLAUDE.md, issues with lint-ignore comments. |

### Sources behind the six lens references

| Standard / source | URL | What was confirmed |
|---|---|---|
| Wikipedia, Fail-safe (tertiary) | https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fail-safe | "Fail-safe means that a device will not endanger lives or property when it fails"; fail-secure, "also called fail-closed", means "access or data will not fall into the wrong hands in a security failure"; example: in a fire, fail-safe systems unlock doors for escape, fail-secure keeps them locked. |
| Green Software Foundation — SCI standards page | https://greensoftware.foundation/standards/sci/ | "SCI = (E × I + M) per R"; cites ISO/IEC 21031:2024 and an ISO accreditation date of April 2024 on its timeline; latest numbered version v1.1, October 2024; "actively developing". |
| Green Software Foundation — SCI specification | https://sci.greensoftware.foundation/ | Version 1.1.0; SCI = C per R, expanded to (O + M) per R with O = E × I and M = TE × TS × RS; E energy in kWh, I region-specific carbon intensity in gCO2eq/kWh, M embodied emissions, R functional unit. The specification page itself does not mention ISO. |
| AWS Well-Architected — Sustainability Pillar | https://docs.aws.amazon.com/wellarchitected/latest/sustainability-pillar/sustainability-pillar.html | Publication date November 6, 2024; the Framework has six pillars: operational excellence, security, reliability, performance efficiency, cost optimization, sustainability. |
| Android — wake locks | https://developer.android.com/develop/background-work/background-tasks/awake/wakelock | "Creating and holding wake locks can have a dramatic impact on the device's battery life. You shouldn't use wake locks if there are any suitable alternatives"; if needed, "hold it for as short a time as possible". |
| MDN — requestAnimationFrame | https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Window/requestAnimationFrame | "`requestAnimationFrame()` calls are paused in most browsers when running in background tabs or hidden `<iframe>`s, in order to improve performance and battery life." |
| GitHub Actions — dependency caching | https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/dependency-caching | `restore-keys` searched in sequential order; runs cannot restore caches created for child or sibling branches but can restore from the current and default branch (and, for a pull request, the base branch); entries not accessed for over 7 days are removed; default limit 10 GB per repository (values as stated on 2026-10-16); the `setup-*` actions create and restore dependency caches for npm, Yarn, pnpm, pip, pipenv, Poetry, Gradle, Maven, RubyGems, Go and NuGet. |
| Docker — build cache | https://docs.docker.com/build/cache/ | "If a layer changes, all other layers that come after it are also affected." |
| Docker — optimize build cache | https://docs.docker.com/build/cache/optimize/ | "try to make expensive steps appear near the beginning of the Dockerfile"; "Steps that change often should appear near the end"; copy the package management files first and install in earlier layers; cache mounts; `.dockerignore`. |
| madge | https://github.com/pahen/madge | `madge --circular path/src/app.js` finds circular dependencies; JavaScript (AMD, CommonJS, ES6); `--orphans`. TypeScript is not listed as supported in the README text read. |
| dependency-cruiser | https://github.com/sverweij/dependency-cruiser | Validates dependencies "against (your own) rules"; detects circular dependencies and orphans; JavaScript, TypeScript, CoffeeScript (ES6, CommonJS, AMD); sample rules use a `forbidden` array. |
| Import Linter | https://github.com/seddonym/import-linter | "Import Linter allows you to impose constraints on the imports between your Python modules."; tagline "Lint your Python architecture." The contract types page returned 404; no contract type is cited. |
| ArchUnit | https://www.archunit.org/ | Checks "dependencies between packages and classes, layers and slices, check for cyclic dependencies and more" of Java code with a plain unit test framework. |
| webpack — tree shaking | https://webpack.js.org/guides/tree-shaking/ | Dead-code elimination that relies on the static structure of ES2015 modules; the `sideEffects` `package.json` property marks files safe to prune; "A side effect is defined as code that performs a special behavior when imported, other than exposing one or more exports"; pitfalls include re-exports with side effects and CSS imported only for effect. The page does not use the word "barrel". |
| Python — `-X importtime` | https://docs.python.org/3/using/cmdline.html | Shows module name, cumulative time and self time for each import; added in 3.7. |
| React — Strict Mode | https://react.dev/reference/react/StrictMode | Runs "one extra setup+cleanup cycle in development for every Effect"; calls component functions and some updaters twice in development; the checks are development-only and do not affect the production build. |
| MDN — Navigator.sendBeacon() | https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Navigator/sendBeacon | Intended for analytics and diagnostics; `unload` and `beforeunload` are unreliable ("especially on mobile, the browser will not fire these events"); send on `visibilitychange` when the page becomes hidden; queued data limited to 64 KiB (65,536 bytes). |
| npm — package.json `engines` | https://docs.npmjs.com/cli/v10/configuring-npm/package-json#engines | Specifies supported Node and npm versions; "advisory only" unless the user has set `engine-strict`; "will only produce warnings" when installed as a dependency; unspecified means any version. |
| Git — gitattributes | https://git-scm.com/docs/gitattributes | `text` normalises line endings to LF in the index on add; `text=auto` lets Git decide; `eol=lf` and `eol=crlf` set the working-tree ending and only take effect with `text` or `text=auto` (and specifying `eol` sets `text`). |
| Web Platform Baseline | https://web.dev/baseline | "Widely available": 30 months after the "newly interoperable" date; core browsers Chrome (desktop and Android), Edge, Firefox (desktop and Android), Safari (macOS and iOS). |
| Browserslist | https://github.com/browserslist/browserslist | A shared config of target browsers and Node versions for tools such as Autoprefixer and Babel, set in `.browserslistrc` or the `browserslist` key of `package.json`; `defaults` is `> 0.5%, last 2 versions, Firefox ESR, not dead` and is used when no config produces a valid result. |

Not read, so not relied on: the Go specification's import-cycle sentence (the fetch truncated before it); the Greptile getting-started page (returned empty); the IEC functional-safety page (HTTP 403). Named only, no fetch: IEC 61508 (the basic functional-safety standard for electrical, electronic and programmable electronic safety-related systems) and ISO 26262 (road vehicles), which `safety-critical.md` names to route a certification question; no clause, level or date from either is cited.

## Verified by direct fetch (2026-10-16) — Claude Code hooks (PreToolUse matcher, MCP tool names, decision control; `agentic-delivery/scripts/comms_guard.py`)

Fetched with a summarising fetch tool on 2026-10-16. The page is 270831 characters long; the slices 0-100000 and 100000-200000 were read and 200000-270831 were not. Each row records only what the summary quoted from the page.

| Standard / source | URL | What was confirmed |
|---|---|---|
| Claude Code — Hooks reference (matcher patterns) | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks | `"*"`, `""` or an omitted matcher matches all. A value made only of letters, digits, `_`, `-`, spaces, `,` and `\|` is an exact string, or a list of exact strings separated by `\|` or `,`. Any other character makes it a JavaScript regular expression, unanchored: "`^Notebook` matches any tool whose name starts with `Notebook`"; "`Edit.*` matches both `Edit` and `NotebookEdit`; wrap the pattern in `^` and `$`, as in `^Edit$`". |
| Claude Code — Hooks reference (match MCP tools) | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks | "MCP tools follow the naming pattern `mcp__<server>__<tool>`"; "`mcp__memory__.*` matches all tools from the `memory` server"; a bare `mcp__memory` "is compared as an exact string and matches no tool"; plugin servers are `mcp__plugin_<plugin-name>_<server-name>__<tool>`. How claude.ai connector servers are named was not in the slices read, so the operating-layer matcher accepts any server segment that contains `Linear` or `linear`. |
| Claude Code — Hooks reference (PreToolUse input) | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks | Common input fields `session_id`, `transcript_path`, `cwd`, `permission_mode`, `hook_event_name`; PreToolUse adds `tool_name`, `tool_input`, `tool_use_id`. The Bash `tool_input` has `command`, `description`, `timeout`, `run_in_background`. An MCP tool's input also carries `mcp_server`, an object with the server's `name` and a `source`. |
| Claude Code — Hooks reference (PreToolUse decision control, exit codes) | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks | `hookSpecificOutput` "requires a `hookEventName` field"; `permissionDecision` is `allow`, `deny`, `ask` or `defer`; `permissionDecisionReason` is shown to Claude for `deny` and to the user for `ask`; precedence across hooks is `deny` > `defer` > `ask` > `allow`. "Exit 0 means success, and is the intended exit code when you print JSON for structured control." "Exit with code 2 to block the action": on PreToolUse Claude sees the stderr message as the denial reason. Other exit codes are non-blocking errors ("The action goes ahead"); "A timed-out `command`, `http`, or `mcp_tool` hook doesn't block the tool call." |
| Claude Code — Hooks reference (`if` field, timeout) | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks | The `if` field is a permission rule such as `"Bash(git *)"`, evaluated only on tool events, "exactly one permission rule"; the default `command` hook timeout is 600 seconds. The operating layer does not use `if`: `comms_guard.py` filters the command itself and registers with `\|\| true`. |

## Verified by direct fetch (2026-10-16) — memory, handoff and docs hygiene (`focus_kit.py`, `references/focus-kit.md`)

Both pages were fetched on 2026-10-16 and each row quotes sentences read from the fetched page text. The context-window sentences come from the text of that page's interactive simulation.

| Standard / source | URL | What was confirmed |
|---|---|---|
| Claude Code — How Claude remembers your project (size and auto-memory limits) | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/memory | CLAUDE.md: "target under 200 lines per CLAUDE.md file"; imports "don't reduce its context cost, because imported files also load at launch" and recurse "with a maximum depth of four hops". Auto memory sits at `~/.claude/projects/<project>/memory/` ("derived from the git repository, so all worktrees and subdirectories within the same repo share one auto memory directory") as a `MEMORY.md` index plus one topic file per memory. "The first 200 lines of `MEMORY.md`, or the first 25KB, whichever comes first, are loaded at the start of every conversation"; the page's comparison table says "Every session (first 200 lines or 25KB)". Topic files are not loaded at startup: Claude reads them on demand. After a write Claude Code reminds Claude to "keep one line per entry, move detail into topic files, and merge or drop stale entries". |
| Claude Code — How Claude remembers your project (what survives `/compact`) | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/memory | "Project-root CLAUDE.md survives compaction: after `/compact`, Claude re-reads it from disk and re-injects it into the session." An instruction that "was given only in conversation" does not persist; the page says to add conversation-only instructions to CLAUDE.md. |
| Claude Code — Explore the context window | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/context-window | "Compaction replaces the conversation with a structured summary. System prompt, CLAUDE.md, memory, and MCP tools reload automatically. Claude Code also re-reads up to five of the files modified most recently and re-injects the skills you invoked. The skill listing does not reload." "After `/compact`, Claude Code re-injects the body of each skill you invoked, capped at 5,000 tokens per skill." |

## Verified by direct fetch (2026-10-16) — sandbox preflight and autonomy advisor (`agentic-delivery/scripts/sandbox_preflight.py`, `lane_guard.py`, `terse_reply_check.py`)

| Standard / source | URL | What was confirmed |
|---|---|---|
| Claude Code — Configure the sandboxed Bash tool | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sandboxing | `sandbox.excludedCommands` entries use `Bash(...)` rule syntax (no wildcard is an exact match; `docker *` matches with or without arguments), match the text of the call (a script that calls `docker` internally does not match, `npx` and absolute paths do not), and a call stays sandboxed when it has a `cd`, a redirect or `$(...)`; an excluded command goes through the regular permission flow; `/sandbox` opens the panel (Mode, Overrides, Config); `sandbox.filesystem.allowWrite` for paths outside the working directory; `sandbox.network.allowedDomains` starts empty; troubleshooting: `jest` needs `--no-watchman`, Go CLIs such as `gh` can fail TLS verification on macOS (exclude `gh *`), `docker` is incompatible, git over SSH fails on macOS (HTTPS remote, or exclude `git fetch *`, `git pull *`, `git push *`), database clients and other non-HTTP tools ignore the proxy (exclude the script, optionally with an ask rule), `allowLocalBinding` is macOS only (on Linux and WSL2 a command's localhost is private), protected paths such as `.claude/skills` stay write-denied. `/sandbox exclude <pattern>` is NOT on this page; it appears only in Claude Code's own sandbox error text, so it is unverified against the docs. |
| Claude Code — Settings reference | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/settings-reference | `sandbox.network.allowUnixSockets` (macOS only; array of socket paths; default blocks every Unix socket), `allowAllUnixSockets`, `allowLocalBinding` (macOS only), `allowMachLookup` (macOS XPC/Mach services; "such as the iOS Simulator or Playwright"; service names not listed), `sandbox.filesystem.allowWrite`, sandbox path prefixes (`~/`, `/`, `./`). |
| Claude Code — Configure permissions | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions | `permissions.allow`/`ask`/`deny`; evaluated deny, then ask, then allow, first match wins; Bash `*` matches any text including spaces and a trailing ` *` also matches the bare command; every subcommand of a compound command must match; `npx` and similar runners are not stripped; built-in read-only commands run without a prompt; `/permissions` lists and edits rules; `WebFetch(domain:...)` rules; `.claude/settings.local.json` is the user's own untracked file, `~/.claude/settings.json` applies to every project, project `permissions.allow` needs the trust dialog. |
| Claude Code — Commands | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/commands | `/sandbox` is listed as "Toggle sandbox mode"; no `exclude` subcommand is documented. |
| Claude Code — Worktrees | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/worktrees | A `.worktreeinclude` file (`.gitignore` syntax) copies gitignored files that match into every worktree Claude Code creates with git, subagent worktrees included; a manual `git worktree add` or another host gets none, which `lane_guard.py`'s banlist copy covers. |
| Claude Code — Hooks reference | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks | Plain-text stdout of a `UserPromptSubmit` or `SessionStart` hook is added to Claude's context; SessionStart matchers `startup`, `resume`, `clear`, `compact`, `fork`. |
| Codex — Agent approvals and security | https://learn.chatgpt.com/docs/agent-approvals-security | `approval_policy = "on-request"` (commands the sandbox allows run without approval) and `sandbox_mode = "workspace-write"` in `~/.codex/config.toml`; network is off by default and enabled with `[sandbox_workspace_write]` `network_access = true`; `[features.network_proxy]` takes a `domains` table (syntax not shown); command allow rules live on a separate Rules page whose syntax is not on this page. |

## Verified by direct fetch (2026-10-19) — usage-cost levers (`agentic-ceo/scripts/token_report.py --advise`, `agentic-delivery/scripts/cost_statusline.py`)

Every page was fetched on 2026-10-19; a lever the pages did not show is not recommended. Fetched and unused, so not cited: the hooks reference (no cost lever found in it). Dollar figures reuse the dated rate rows above; a model with no rate row is `UNPRICED`.

| Standard / source | URL | What was confirmed |
|---|---|---|
| Claude Code — Manage costs effectively | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/costs | `/usage` shows the session cost and, after the first response, a `Prompt cache (main)` line (request count, share of input tokens served from cache, misses, whether the cache is warm; needs v2.1.251 or later). Keep CLAUDE.md under 200 lines. Extended thinking tokens are billed as output tokens. Source for the `token_report.py --advise` levers `/clear`, `/compact`, `/context`, `/mcp` and MCP-server pruning. |
| Claude Code — Commands | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/commands | Built-in commands used as levers: `/usage` (also `/cost`, `/stats`), `/context`, `/compact`, `/clear`, `/autocompact`, `/model`, `/effort`, `/statusline`, `/mcp`, `/memory`. `/autocompact` is named only because this page lists it. |
| Claude Code — Settings reference | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/settings-reference | Keys read by `--advise` as already-set checks: `autoCompactWindow` (valid range 100K to 1M; `/autocompact` and `--autocompact` set it per model or per launch; `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW` wins over the others), `bashOutputMaxChars`, `statusLine`. `modelPricing` is a managed-settings key. No settings key for tool search is documented, so none is read. MCP keys (index rows, scope "Any file"): `disabledMcpjsonServers` "Reject specific servers from a project's `.mcp.json`"; `enabledMcpjsonServers` "Approve specific servers from a project's `.mcp.json`"; `enableAllProjectMcpServers` "Approve every server in project `.mcp.json` files without a prompt". Verified 2026-10-19. |
| Claude Code — Settings files and precedence | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/settings | User (`~/.claude/settings.json`), project (`.claude/settings.json`) and local (`.claude/settings.local.json`) scopes; `statusLine` is accepted in user or project settings. `--advise --project` reads these three plus `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`. Project local `.claude/settings.local.json` is a settings scope ("You, in this one project only") that Claude Code applies over the committed `.claude/settings.json`, so a `statusLine` written there is read like one in project settings. Verified 2026-10-19. "Lists merge instead of overriding": a list key set in more than one file is combined, not replaced (model-list keys excepted); `--advise` merges list keys the same way. Verified 2026-10-19. |
| Claude Code — Environment variables | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/env-vars | Env levers read as already-set checks: `CLAUDE_AUTOCOMPACT_PCT_OVERRIDE`, `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW`, `BASH_MAX_OUTPUT_LENGTH`, `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL`, `ENABLE_TOOL_SEARCH`, `MAX_MCP_OUTPUT_TOKENS`; `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` "Name of the model setting to use" (verified 2026-10-19). |
| Claude Code — Model configuration | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/model-config | `/model`, `/effort` and the `effortLevel` and `model` settings are the model and effort levers; `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL` sets the model for subagents. |
| Claude Code — Customize your status line | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/statusline | `statusLine` is `{"type": "command", "command": ...}` (optional `padding`, `refreshInterval`) in user or project settings; the command gets a JSON session object on stdin with `cost.total_cost_usd` (client-side list-price estimate, may differ from the bill), `context_window.used_percentage` and `prompt_cache.hit_ratio` / `last_miss_cause`. Output shows only after exit code 0; a non-zero exit or empty output blanks the line. Runs under the same workspace-trust rule as settings hooks. The page does not say which environment variables or working directory the command gets, so the command resolves `cost_statusline.py` from `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`, else the git top level of its working directory, else that directory, and prints nothing if the script is not there. |
| Claude Code — Connect Claude Code to tools via MCP | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/mcp | Project-scope servers live in `.mcp.json` under a top-level `mcpServers` key. Tool search defers MCP tool definitions by default; `ENABLE_TOOL_SEARCH=false` loads them upfront. Claude Code warns when one MCP tool output passes 10,000 tokens; `MAX_MCP_OUTPUT_TOKENS` defaults to 25,000. |
| Claude Code — Memory | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/memory | CLAUDE.md size guidance (under 200 lines) and `/memory`; the same guidance is on the costs page. |
| Claude Code — How Claude Code uses prompt caching | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/prompt-caching | Each model has its own cache, so a `/model` switch re-reads the context uncached; compaction also rewrites the cached prefix. Basis for the `cache-switch` and `ctx-drops` advice rules. |

**By name only, not verified:** that tsx's IPC pipe is the cause of `listen EPERM` under the macOS sandbox comes from field reports, not from a docs page; the docs confirm only that the macOS sandbox blocks every Unix socket by default. The Playwright `allowMachLookup` service names are not documented.

## Verified by direct fetch (2026-10-21) — usage-optimization levers added to `token_report.py --advise` (`idle-resume`, `cache-bust`, `effort`)

Every page was fetched on 2026-10-21. A lever these pages did not show is not recommended. Fetched and not used as a lever: the `idleCompaction` setting (the settings-reference fetch showed only its index row, so its default and behavior are unconfirmed), and `skillListingMaxDescChars` (documented, but no transcript usage, timestamp or model field measures it, so no rule fires it).

| Claude Code — Manage costs effectively | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/costs | "Clear between tasks: Use `/clear` to start fresh when switching to unrelated work"; `/rename` before clearing and `/resume` to return. "Thinking tokens are billed as output tokens"; lower the effort level "with `/effort` or in `/model`". "Cache misses: your first message after a break longer than the cache lifetime misses the cache and reprocesses your full context." `/usage` `Prompt cache (main)` line names the likely cause of the last miss (v2.1.251 and v2.1.260). Basis for `idle-resume` and `effort`. |
| Claude Code — How Claude Code uses prompt caching | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/prompt-caching | "Actions that invalidate the cache": switching models, changing effort level (per-effort cache on most models; the cache is kept on Opus 5.5, Sonnet 5.5, Haiku 5.5 and Fable 5.1 with an API key or subscription), fast mode, connecting or removing an MCP server (when tool definitions load upfront), enabling or disabling a plugin that provides MCP servers, denying an entire tool, compaction (an expected rebuild, like tool-result clearing), many images, upgrading. "Actions that keep the cache": editing files, editing CLAUDE.md mid-session. Cache TTL is five minutes or one hour (`promptCacheTtl`, `CLAUDE_CODE_PROMPT_CACHE_TTL`). "After a break longer than the cache lifetime ... the summarization request reprocesses the full history as uncached input", so `/compact` costs the most after a long break. Basis for `cache-bust` and `idle-resume`. |
| Claude Code — Model configuration | https://code.claude.com/docs/en/model-config | `/effort` takes a level name, `auto` or `status`; levels `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh`, `max` on supported models ("Models not listed here do not support effort"); the `effortLevel` setting and `CLAUDE_CODE_EFFORT_LEVEL` env var; the saved per-model level lives under `modelSettings`. "You are charged for all thinking tokens generated, even when collapsed or redacted." The `effort` rule stays silent when any of these is set. |

Section 3. |
| GitHub Actions — OpenID Connect concepts | https://docs.github.com/en/actions/concepts/security/openid-connect | OIDC trust is established in the cloud provider for specific workflows and roles; the provider checks the token's subject and other claims against configured conditions. Section 3. |
| GitHub Actions — OIDC reference | https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/security/oidc | Audience and subject claims are used together to scope cloud role access; subjects can filter by environment, branch or tag; the job must grant `id-token: write`; "you must define at least one condition, so that untrusted repositories can't request access tokens." Section 3. |
| SLSA v1.0 — About | https://slsa.dev/spec/v1.0/about | "SLSA does not address organizations that intentionally produce malicious software"; SLSA "is only one part of a thorough approach to supply chain security." Basis for "valid provenance is not trust". Section 3. |

Section 4. |

Section 4. |

Section 5. |

Section 5. |

Section 2. |
